# ASSET_MANIFEST - Club & Grub

Reference for every file under `assets/`. Generated by `docs/art/pipeline/build_manifest.py` from the registry that the
asset pipeline writes while it builds the files, so the numbers below are measured, not typed by hand.
Proof screenshots composed only from these files: `docs/art/mock_jungle.png`, `mock_cave.png`, `mock_ice.png`,
`mock_volcano.png`, `mock_feast.png`, `mock_boss_brute.png`, `mock_boss_colossus.png` (each 2560x1440 = base viewport x4).

## 1. Art direction and global conventions

**One artist, one look.** Hero, enemies, bosses, NPCs, all terrain, props, parallax layers, HUD, bitmap fonts and most FX come
from a single purpose-made kit: *Superpowers Asset Packs - Prehistoric Platformer* by Pixel-boy / Sparklin Labs (CC0). It is
chunky cartoon pixel art with a 2 px dark-brown outline (`#272018`), flat cel shading and a warm palette. Everything added from
other packs was chosen or processed to sit inside that look (dark 2 px outline, flat shading): 16 px food / gem icons at
integer 2x, 32 px giant food at 2x with an added `#272018` outline, carved-stone touch buttons at 2x recoloured to sandstone.

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Base viewport | **640 x 360 art px** (16:9) = **320 x 180 logical px** x `ART_SCALE` **2** (PHYSICS.md 15.3) |
| Tile size | **32 x 32 art px** = 16 x 16 logical px; 20 x 11.25 tiles visible (the original shows 20 x 11) |
| Hero size | body 44 x 56 art px (22 x 28 logical) = 15.6 % of the screen height, i.e. about 1/6 as in Prehistorik 2 |
| Integer window scales | 1280x720 = x2, 1920x1080 = x3, 2560x1440 = x4, 3840x2160 = x6 |
| Texture filter | Nearest everywhere, no mipmaps, no texture compression artefacts (import PNGs lossless) |
| Sheet layout | uniform grid, frames numbered row-major from 0 (`frame = row * columns + column`), ready for `Sprite2D.hframes / vframes` |
| Actor pivot | **(cell_w / 2, cell_h - 16)** = the feet point of PHYSICS.md 2 (bottom-centre). `centered = false`, `offset = (-cell_w/2, -(cell_h-16))`; `flip_h` mirrors correctly because the pivot is horizontally centred. Bosses at 2x use `cell_h - 32`. |
| Facing | every actor sheet faces **right**; exceptions: `bat` (front view) and `colossus` (faces left, lives in the right wall) |
| Item / object / prop pivot | bottom-centre of the cell unless the table says otherwise |
| Collision boxes | given in logical px around the feet point: `left = x - w/2`, `top = y - h` (PHYSICS.md 2.1 box test). They are suggestions measured from the art. |
| Pixel density | native 1x of the anchor pack. Deliberate integer upscales (nearest neighbour): bosses 2x, 16 px icons 2x, giant food 2x, touch buttons 2x. Nothing is scaled by a non-integer factor. |

Why 640 x 360 and not 320x180 / 384x216 / 480x270: the chosen art is drawn at twice the density of 16 px-tile packs (hero 45x57 px,
32 px terrain modules). At 640x360 it reproduces the original framing exactly (20 tile columns, hero 1/6 of the height); at 480x270 only
15 x 8.4 tiles would fit and the hero would fill 21 % of the height. Game logic still runs in the original's 320x180 logical pixels:
simulate in logical px and multiply positions by `ART_SCALE = 2` when placing sprites, exactly as PHYSICS.md 15.3 describes.

File naming: lower_snake_case; `_b` = second palette of the same sheet layout; audio `_a.._d` = interchangeable variants.

## 2. GAMEPLAY.md archetype -> chosen sprite

| Archetype (GAMEPLAY 5.2 / 12.2) | Role | Sprite sheet(s) | Animations to use | Suggested skins per world |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 Sky dropper | Dropper | `sprites/enemies/egg_kid.png` (+`_b`) | fall = `roll` (egg), landing = `land`, then `walk` | all worlds; `_b` palette for Expert |
| 2 Yo-yo dangler | Dangler | `sprites/enemies/bat.png` (+`_b`) | `hang` (frame 13); draw the thread from the top centre of the sprite; `dead` | cave, jungle canopy, ice cave (`_b`) |
| 3 Ceiling dropper -> chaser | Lurker | `sprites/enemies/insect.png` (+`_b`) | `hang` while intangible, descend, then `walk` (chase) | cave, volcano (`_b`) |
| 4 Pendulum | Swinger | `sprites/enemies/bat_b.png` | `hang` rotated by the pendulum angle, or `swoop` | cave, jungle |
| 5 Sentry diver | Stinger | `sprites/enemies/insect.png` / `insect_b.png` | `fly` (hover and dive) | jungle, volcano |
| 6 Clever flyer | Harrier | `sprites/enemies/pterodactyl.png` (+`_b`) | `fly`; `screech` when it swoops | jungle, ice (`_b`) |
| 7 Kamikaze diver | Dart | `sprites/enemies/pterodactyl_b.png`, `bat.png` | `dive` (pterodactyl) / `swoop` (bat) | ice, volcano |
| 8 Hopper | Hopper | `sprites/enemies/mini_rex.png` (+`_b`), `lizard_b.png` | `idle` (pause), `walk` frame as jump pose / `hop`, `attack` on contact | jungle (orange), ice (`_b` blue) |
| 9 Patroller, ground | Walker | `sprites/enemies/turtle.png` (+`_b`) | `walk`; follows slopes | all worlds |
| 9 Patroller, air | Glider | `sprites/enemies/bat.png`, `pterodactyl.png` | `fly` | cave / jungle |
| 10 Burrower | Digger | `sprites/enemies/lizard.png` (+`_b`) | rise / sink = reveal the `idle` frame bottom-up with a clip rect, `hop` while walking | cave, volcano; boss minions |
| 11 Arc leaper | Leaper | `sprites/enemies/dragon.png` (+`_b`) | leap = `attack` frames 7-8 (stretched lunge), glide = `glide` | volcano (purple), ice water pits (`_b` teal) |
| 12 Edge rusher | Charger | `sprites/enemies/rival.png`, `rex.png` (+`_b`) | `walk` at 16-20 fps | rival caveman everywhere; rex as heavy charger in worlds 3-4 |
| stationary hazard ('red snake') | Snapper | `sprites/enemies/plant.png` (+`_b`) | `idle`, `windup` + `bite` when the hero is within 84 art px | jungle, feast |
| 1 Decoration | - | `tiles/jungle/props/vine_a.png`, `vine_b.png`, `tiles/cave/props/critter_shadow.png` | static | - |
| Boss: Brute (6.1) | giant | `sprites/bosses/brute.png`, `brute_enraged.png` | `idle` (watch), `taunt` (chest beat), `jump` + `land`, `roll` (leap), `attack` (punch), `pound`, `hurt`, `death` | level 2-2; `brute_enraged` when under 60 hit points or for the Expert rematch |
| Boss: Wall Colossus (6.3) | wall statue | `sprites/bosses/colossus.png` | `idle`, `spit` (rock), `slam` (ceiling drop cue), `hurt`, `rage`, `broken` | level 4-2 |
| (stretch) Rooted Guardian (6.2) | stationary puncher | not built - reuse `plant.png` at 2x or `rex.png` | - | optional mid-boss of 4-1 |
| Tally companion (3.7) | NPC | `sprites/npc/companion.png` | `idle`, `walk`, `hurt` (crying on game over) | - |

## 3. Player

| File | Size | Frame | Grid (cols x rows) | Pivot | Facing | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sprites/player/hero.png` | 1408x784 | 176x112 | 8x7 | (88, 96) | right | weapon variant: club |
| `sprites/player/hero_axe.png` | 1408x784 | 176x112 | 8x7 | (88, 96) | right | weapon variant: axe |
| `sprites/player/hero_boomerang.png` | 1408x784 | 176x112 | 8x7 | (88, 96) | right | weapon variant: boomerang |
| `sprites/player/hero_hammer.png` | 1408x784 | 176x112 | 8x7 | (88, 96) | right | weapon variant: hammer |

All four hero sheets share one layout; swap the texture when the weapon changes (`hero.png` = club, the default).
Thrown weapons (axe, boomerang): the release frames show an empty hand - spawn `sprites/fx/projectile_<weapon>.png` there.

| Animation | Frames | fps | Loop | Use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| idle | 0-5 | 8 | yes | standing, club on the shoulder |
| walk | 6-13 | 12 | yes | walk and run (use 12 fps walking, 16 fps at full speed) |
| jump | 14-16 | 10 | no | rising; hold the last frame |
| fall | 17-19 | 10 | yes | falling |
| land | 20 | 1 | no | landing squash after a long fall; also the skid pose |
| crouch | 21 | 1 | no | Down held (crouch box) |
| crawl | 22-23 | 6 | yes | crawl shuffle (Down + direction) |
| roll | 24-26 | 14 | yes | alternative crawl / long-fall tumble (ball) |
| attack | 27-30 | 16 | no | forward swing; frame 29 is the hit frame (slash arc drawn in) |
| attack_up | 31-32 | 12 | no | Up + attack, overhead strike; frame 32 hits |
| attack_crouch | 33-34 | 12 | no | Down + attack, low strike; frame 34 hits |
| attack_air | 35-36 | 12 | no | swing while airborne; frame 36 hits |
| hurt | 37-38 | 8 | yes | thrown back (22 ticks) |
| death | 39-43 | 8 | no | death toss; 39-40 while airborne, 41-43 if you let him land |
| climb | 44-47 | 6 | yes | ladders / vines (back view, optional mechanic) |
| victory | 48-49 | 4 | yes | level complete, club raised |
| glide | 50-51 | 4 | yes | hanging under `sprites/objects/glider.png` |

Suggested collision boxes (logical px, w x h, centred on the feet point): stand / walk / jump **22 x 28**; crouch / crawl **24 x 22**;
roll **18 x 18**. PHYSICS.md 2.1 lists the original's 32 x 35 and 40 x 30: keep those for a 1:1 feel test, the art fits inside them.
Club reach in the hit frame: 44 logical px in front of the feet point, 8-30 px above the ground (the baked slash arc).

Edits: re-packed 97x71 cells into 176x112; club composited behind the body in idle/walk/jump/fall/crouch (carried on the shoulder) and in the attack frames; attack_up / attack_crouch / attack_air / victory assembled from existing poses; slash arc added.

## 4. Enemies

Eleven enemy types; ten of them also come in a second palette (`_b`: turtle green, mini_rex blue, lizard red, dragon teal, insect cyan, bat blue, pterodactyl green, plant blue, egg_kid brown, rex grey) for Expert-only spawns or biome variety. No projectiles (the original has none). Enemies die by being knocked off-screen in an arc: use `dead` / `hit` as the tumbling pose. Feast mode swaps the sprite for a cell of `sprites/items/food.png`.

## 5. Bosses

Both bosses are integer 2x upscales so that they tower over the hero (Brute body 110 x 122 art px, Colossus 208 x 190).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sprites/bosses/brute.png` | 2016x1056 | 288x176 | 7x6 | (144, 144) | right | 55x61 | BOSS 1 'the Brute' (gorilla archetype, GAMEPLAY 6.1). Head = top 30 logical px of the body box (weak point); taunt = chest beating, roll = leap, pound = ground pound, attack = punch |
| `sprites/bosses/brute_enraged.png` | 2016x1056 | 288x176 | 7x6 | (144, 144) | right | 55x61 | palette variant of the Brute for the tougher second encounter / low-health phase |
| `sprites/bosses/colossus.png` | 1560x672 | 260x224 | 6x3 | (260, 208) | left | 104x95 | BOSS 2 'the Wall Colossus' (minotaur archetype, GAMEPLAY 6.3). Pivot = bottom-RIGHT corner of the cell (put it on the arena's right wall, floor level). Head rectangles per pose are listed below the table |

- `sprites/bosses/brute.png`: **idle** 0-5 @8 fps loop; **walk** 6-13 @12 fps loop; **jump** 14-16 @10 fps once; **land** 17 @1 fps once; **roll** 18-20 @14 fps loop; **crouch** 21 @1 fps once; **attack** 22-25 @12 fps once; **hurt** 26-27 @8 fps loop; **death** 28-32 @8 fps once; **taunt** 33-36 @6 fps loop; **pound** 37-40 @8 fps loop
- `sprites/bosses/brute_enraged.png`: **idle** 0-5 @8 fps loop; **walk** 6-13 @12 fps loop; **jump** 14-16 @10 fps once; **land** 17 @1 fps once; **roll** 18-20 @14 fps loop; **crouch** 21 @1 fps once; **attack** 22-25 @12 fps once; **hurt** 26-27 @8 fps loop; **death** 28-32 @8 fps once; **taunt** 33-36 @6 fps loop; **pound** 37-40 @8 fps loop
- `sprites/bosses/colossus.png`: **idle** 0-5 @6 fps loop; **spit** 6-9 @8 fps once; **slam** 10-11 @6 fps once; **hurt** 12-13 @8 fps loop; **rage** 14-15 @8 fps loop; **broken** 16 @1 fps once

Brute hit zones (logical px relative to the feet point, facing right; mirror x when it faces left): body x -29..+26, y -61..0; head (weak point, bounce-safe) x -29..+18, y -61..-31; punching fist during `attack` frames 23-25: x +40..+70, y -34..-20.
Colossus: place the pivot (bottom-right of the cell) on the arena floor at the right wall; the last 64 art px of the cell are the wall rim. Head rectangle per pose (art px inside the 260x224 cell): `idle` (48, 68)-(140, 130); `spit` / `rage` (88, 18)-(190, 110); `slam` (14, 88)-(110, 160); `hurt` (80, 18)-(190, 100). A single generous zone that works for every pose: x 14..190, y 18..130. Rock spawn point (open jaws in `spit`): cell (92, 70).

## 10. Tiles (assets/tiles)

### 10.1 Terrain atlases

Every terrain file is a 256 x 160 atlas of 8 x 5 tiles (32 x 32 art px) with the **same index meaning**, so one TileSet layout /
one collision table serves all of them. Tiles 0-34 come from one terrain set of the pack; 35-37 are background-wall tiles; 38-39 spikes.

| Index | Name | Meaning | Collision |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `top_left` | ground surface, left outer corner | solid |
| 1 | `top` | ground surface (repeatable) | solid |
| 2 | `top_right` | ground surface, right outer corner | solid |
| 3 | `cap_left` | alternate surface (2-tile pillar / ledge cap), left half with left edge | solid |
| 4 | `cap_right` | alternate surface cap, right half with right edge | solid |
| 5 | `slope45_up_right` | steep slope rising to the right: height 0 -> 16 logical px | slope |
| 6 | `slope45_up_left` | steep slope rising to the left: height 16 -> 0 logical px | slope |
| 7 | `block` | single framed block (breakable block / hidden-spot look) | solid |
| 8 | `left` | left wall edge | solid |
| 9 | `fill` | inner fill | solid |
| 10 | `right` | right wall edge | solid |
| 11 | `gentle_up_right_low` | gentle slope rising to the right, low half: height 0 -> 8 logical px | slope |
| 12 | `gentle_up_right_high` | gentle slope rising to the right, high half: height 8 -> 16 | slope |
| 13 | `gentle_up_left_high` | gentle slope rising to the left, high half: height 16 -> 8 | slope |
| 14 | `gentle_up_left_low` | gentle slope rising to the left, low half: height 8 -> 0 | slope |
| 15 | `fill_inset` | fill with an inset panel (scenery; suggested look for hidden bonus spots) | solid |
| 16 | `bottom_left` | underside, left outer corner | solid |
| 17 | `bottom` | underside (repeatable) | solid |
| 18 | `bottom_right` | underside, right outer corner | solid |
| 19 | `under_slope45_right` | fill placed directly below slope45_up_right | solid |
| 20 | `under_slope45_left` | fill placed directly below slope45_up_left | solid |
| 21 | `under_gentle_right_low` | fill placed below gentle_up_right_low | solid |
| 22 | `under_gentle_right_high` | fill placed below gentle_up_right_high | solid |
| 23 | `fill_b` | inner fill, variant with detail | solid |
| 24 | `under_gentle_left_high` | fill placed below gentle_up_left_high | solid |
| 25 | `under_gentle_left_low` | fill placed below gentle_up_left_low | solid |
| 26 | `bottom_b` | underside, variant | solid |
| 27 | `oneway_left` | thin one-way platform, left end (upper 16 px of the cell) | one-way |
| 28 | `oneway_mid` | thin one-way platform, middle (repeatable) | one-way |
| 29 | `oneway_right` | thin one-way platform, right end | one-way |
| 30 | `hang_left` | hanging fringe under a ledge, left end (upper 16 px, decor) | decor |
| 31 | `hang_mid` | hanging fringe, middle (decor) | decor |
| 32 | `hang_right` | hanging fringe, right end (decor) | decor |
| 33 | `hang_cap` | hanging rounded cap (decor) | decor |
| 34 | `small_block` | 16 px block, bottom-left of the cell (decor / debris) | decor |
| 35 | `back_fill` | background wall, plain (no collision, drawn behind actors) | decor |
| 36 | `back_deco_a` | background wall with a star crack (decor) | decor |
| 37 | `back_deco_b` | background wall with a crack and a hole (decor) | decor |
| 38 | `spikes_floor` | floor spikes, deadly from the top (lower 16 px of the cell) | hazard |
| 39 | `spikes_ceiling` | ceiling spikes, deadly from below (upper 16 px of the cell) | hazard |

Usage rules:

- Outlines are part of the art: use `top*` where air is above, `left` / `right` where air is beside, `bottom*` where air is below, `fill` inside. No inner-corner tiles are needed: where ground meets a rising wall, put `fill` in the wall column at ground level and `left` / `right` above it.
- Slopes: `slope45_*` rises 16 logical px over one tile; `gentle_*` rises 8 px per tile (low + high halves). Put the matching `under_*` tile directly below each slope tile. Slope tiles are solid from the top only along their surface line (store the left / right heights given above in the HEIGHT layer).
- One-way platforms occupy the upper 16 art px (8 logical px) of their cell: collision line at the cell top, passable from below.
- `spikes_floor` / `spikes_ceiling` are deadly; `hang_*`, `small_block`, `back_*` have no collision.
- `fill_inset` is ordinary scenery with an inset panel: the suggested look for hidden bonus spots (swap to `back_fill` or `fill` when the spot is used up).

### 10.4 Props

Single-image scenery, pivot = bottom-centre (ceiling pieces: top-centre, noted). No collision unless used as a gate / platform. All cut from the pack's `tileset-1.png`; ice and volcano versions are palette recolours, cave rocks are gradient-mapped to the violet rock, crystals are recoloured tusks.

**jungle** (`assets/tiles/jungle/props/`, 31 files): boulders 64x32, bush_big 62x26, bush_dark 52x23, bush_light 52x23, egg_nest 87x62, egg_nest_broken 87x40, fern 32x22, flower 16x14, foliage_block 32x48, foliage_clump 32x35, grass_a 29x21, grass_b 29x21, grass_strip_dark 62x15, grass_strip_light 62x15, leaf 22x18, moss_fringe 93x20, mound_grass 61x20, mound_leafy 32x22, rock_big 64x52, rock_grass 32x30, root_arch 80x63, skull_big 54x47, sprouts 27x16, tree_canopy_dark 112x78, tree_canopy_light 112x70, tree_trunk 78x48, tree_trunk_small 48x32, treetop_stack 32x48, vine_a 18x88, vine_b 32x96, vine_branch 75x23.

## 11. Parallax backgrounds (assets/backgrounds)

Every layer is 360 px high and already positioned vertically: draw all layers of a set at y = 0, back to front, and repeat them
horizontally (`texture_repeat`). Width = loop length. `scroll` = suggested horizontal parallax factor (camera x multiplier).

HUD layout used in the mocks (art px, 640x360): lives icon at (8, 6) + `x2` at (58, 16); score at (112, 16); three hearts at x 270 / 304 / 338, y 8;
bonus letters at x 430 + 40 n, y 4-10 (staggered); boss pips from (10, 334) every 18 px. Keep a 16 px safe margin on phones.

## 13. Audio (assets/audio)

All 79 files are byte-identical copies of the files recorded in the staging area (WAV 16-bit or OGG Vorbis); nothing was edited or re-encoded by the art pipeline.
Six music tracks exist only as WAV at the source and were transcoded to OGG Vorbis (quality 0.6, sample-exact length) by the audio scout; they are marked in the table.
Apply `volume_db` in the AudioStreamPlayer (masters differ by up to 20 dB between authors). The `volume_db` values below are measured, not guessed: see 13.4.
Looping files: set the import flag `loop = true` (OGG) / loop mode Forward (WAV).

### 13.3 Original cue slots -> files (GAMEPLAY 10.1)

| Slot | Original use | File |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | hammer swing; kill-all / grenade explosion | `club_swing_b.wav`; `explosion_a.wav` |
| 1 | heavy hurt: skull, boss hit, boss projectile; lights off | `player_hurt_b.wav` |
| 2 | enemy killed by a weapon; Colossus roar when hit | `enemy_death_a.wav` / `_b`; `boss_roar_a.wav` |
| 3 | bounce on an enemy or boss head | `spring_bounce_a.wav` |
| 4 | big pick-up: giant bonus, heart, 1UP | `gem_pickup_a.wav`, `energy_refill_a.wav`, `one_up_a.wav` |
| 5 | club swing | `club_swing_a.wav` |
| 7 | hero death | `player_death_a.wav` + music `player_death_a.ogg` |
| 8 | normal pick-up; boss hit by a weapon; tally item | `food_pickup_a..d.wav`; `boss_hit_a.wav`; `tally_tick_a.wav` |
| 9 | hero hurt by an enemy | `player_hurt_a.wav` |
| 10 | axe throw | `projectile_throw_b.wav` |

### 13.4 Loudness, peaks and the mix

Measured by `tools/audio_loudness.py` (decodes every file to float exactly as the game's decoders do, so values above 0 dBFS are visible; ITU-R BS.1770-4 K-weighting via pyloudnorm; true peak 4x oversampled). `scripts/core/audio_table.gd` holds the resulting `volume_db` per file; run `tools/audio_loudness.py --write` after adding or replacing a file.

- **Music** (and jingles) play at **-18 LUFS integrated**. The masters ranged from -6.9 LUFS (`level_extra_a`) to -20.4 LUFS (`bonus_room_b`), a 13.5 LU spread; 9 of the 21 music files decode above 0 dBFS sample peak (up to +1.3 dBFS, `level_cave_a.ogg`) and 13 above 0 dBTP true peak (up to +2.4 dBTP, `password_screen_a.ogg`).
- **Effects** play at **-14 LUFS over their loudest 200 ms** (short-window loudness: the EBU 400 ms momentary window over-rates the boost very short clicks need), i.e. a few LU above the music so that they cut through it. Per-role offsets: footsteps -10 LU, fire / lava ambience loops -12 LU and the blizzard loop -8 LU (loops measured by integrated loudness), menu cursor -3 LU, tally tick -4 LU, scenery hits -2 LU, boss roar and boss defeated +2 LU.
- **No clipping**: every gain is capped so that the file's true peak stays at or below **-1 dBTP** (this limits `land_a`, `menu_move_a`, `club_hit_a` and `breakable_smash_ice_a`, which therefore play slightly below their target), and the `Master` bus has a hard limiter (ceiling -0.5 dB) for sounds that overlap.
- The user's volume sliders (music 0.8 by default = -1.9 dB) apply on top of these values.

## 14. Generated or modified assets (edit log)

Everything below was produced by the scripts in `docs/art/pipeline/` (Python + Pillow, nearest-neighbour only, palette-preserving recolours applied per unique colour). Files not listed are byte-identical or lossless re-packs of the source art.

| What | Files | How |
|---|---|---|
| Hero with weapons | `sprites/player/hero*.png` | the pack's club / hammer / axe / boomerang item sprite rotated about its grip (upscale-rotate-point-sample, outline rebuilt) and composited behind the body in every carry and attack frame; slash arc from `fx/effects/1.png` |
| New hero poses | same | `attack_up`, `attack_crouch`, `attack_air`, `victory`, `crawl`, `glide` assembled from existing frames |
| Hang poses | `sprites/enemies/bat*.png`, `insect*.png` | one frame flipped vertically and re-anchored |
| Mirrored sheets | `turtle*.png`, `rex*.png` | source faces left; mirrored so that every actor faces right |
| Bosses | `sprites/bosses/*.png` | integer 2x; `taunt` / `pound` loops built from existing frames; Colossus = T-rex gradient-mapped to stone / lava-glow / dead ramps, cropped at the wall line, obsidian rim added |
| Brute sheets re-packed | `sprites/bosses/brute.png`, `brute_enraged.png` | lossless re-pack by the enemies module from 8 x 6 cells (2304 px wide) to 7 x 6 cells (2016x1056) to stay under the 2048 px texture limit of low-end Android GPUs; cell size, pivot and frame numbers (0-40, row-major) are unchanged, only `hframes` is now 7 |
| Terrain atlases | `tiles/*/terrain*.png` | re-cut to the common 40-tile layout; right-hand pieces mirrored; `ice/terrain_rock`, `volcano/terrain_obsidian`, `feast/terrain_icing` are gradient-mapped recolours |
| Liquids | `tiles/common/lava.png`, `ice_water.png` | exact colour swap of the water tiles |
| Biome props | `tiles/ice/props`, `tiles/volcano/props`, cave rocks, crystals, icicle | palette recolours of the jungle / neutral props |
| Objects | `exit_totem.png`, `checkpoint.png`, `glider.png`, `breakable_block_*.png`, `platform_ice.png`, `platform_stone.png` | composites / recolours described in section 8 |
| FX | `splash_water.png`, `splash_lava.png`, `falling_ember.png`, `projectile_*.png` | recolours and lossless 90 degree rotations |
| Backgrounds | `backgrounds/**` | cropped to 360 px, pre-positioned, mirrored copy appended for seamless looping; ice / volcano / feast / cave sets are gradient-mapped recolours of the pack's jungle and rock layers |
| Icons at 2x | `sprites/items/food.png` (0-39), `treasure.png`, parts of `pickups.png`, `ui/hud_heart.png`, `ui/icons.png` | 16 px icons scaled 2x |
| Giant food | `sprites/items/giant_bonus.png` | 32 px sprites scaled 2x + 2 px `#272018` outline |
| Touch buttons | `ui/touch_buttons.png`, `ui/touch_pause.png` | 2x, gradient-mapped to sandstone, pressed row generated |
| UI composites | `ui/panel.png`, `ui/bar_fill.png`, `ui/title_logo.png`, `ui/world_map_background.png`, `icon.png`, `splash.png`, `fonts/font_title.png` | re-cut / composed from pack pieces |

23 source packs, 21 tier A (CC0), 2 tier B (SIL OFL fonts), 0 tier C. Details and licence texts: `CREDITS.md`, `assets/licenses/`.

## 16. Known gaps

- No sound was auditioned by ear (the pipeline has no audio output): picks rest on the audio scout's measurements; a human listen-through of `assets/audio` is still required, and `volume_db` values are starting points.
- The Rooted Guardian (optional tree-stump mid-boss, GAMEPLAY 6.2) has no dedicated sprite.
- Bosses are pixel-doubled (4 px outlines next to the 2 px outlines of everything else); no boss-sized art exists in the anchor style.
- The hero has no dedicated skid, out-of-breath or idle-fidget animation: use `land` for the skid and `idle` at double speed for panting.
- Terrain has no drawn inner-corner tiles and one top variant per set; long flat stretches repeat every 32 px (break them up with props and `fill_b`).
- Hang-glider: one static overlay sprite (no inflate / fold animation).
- Feast Land has no prop folder of its own: decorate it with `sprites/items/giant_bonus.png` cells and food sprites.
- World-map level markers and the Beginner-wall castle picture are not drawn; compose them from `ui/world_map_background.png`, `ui/icons.png` and village props.
- `font_hud.png` covers ASCII and a few Western accents only; use `press_start_2p.ttf` / `pixelify_sans.ttf` for other languages.

## 17. Expansion 2.0 "The Far Shore"

Every file added for 2.0 (DESIGN.md F.1). Generated by `docs/art/expansion/pipeline/build_expansion.py` from `docs/art/expansion/pipeline/registry_expansion.json`: art-A's files (hero colours, co-op and Book II objects, items, effects, UI, liquids, versus art) are built by that pipeline; art-B's world files and the audio owner's sounds and music arrive with their rows through `.tools/asset_candidates/expansion/_handover/` and are merged here (owner column; the audio rows are checked against the files by sha256). The conventions of section 1 hold unchanged: anchor style, 2 px `#272018` outline, nearest-neighbour, integer scales only, every sheet <= 2048 px. Sections 17.10-17.12 follow the edit log so that the numbers 17.1-17.9 stay what the code cites. Proof sheets: `docs/art/expansion/*.png` (hero colours `heroes_*.png`, `hero_patterns*.png`, `hero_palette_swatches.png`; objects `coop_objects_frames.png`, `coop_scene_canyon.png`, `coop_scene_cave.png`, `book2_objects.png`, `book2_scene_fen.png`, `book2_scene_gulch.png`; liquids `liquids_tar_floor.png`; versus `versus_totem_ring.png`; readability `mp_indicators.png`, `eggs_p1_p4.png`; phase 2: the map page `far_shore_map.png`, the paintings `paintings_slab_mural.png`, versus screens and HUD `versus_screens.png`, Feast Lands `feast_d_e.png`, world objects `world_objects_p2.png`).

### 17.1 Hero colours P1-P4: palette LUTs and loincloth patterns

Player colours are a palette swap in a shader (TECH_AUDIT 5.3: one shared `ShaderMaterial` per slot, no baked sheets; +0 MB). P1 with `yellow` + pattern `spots` reproduces 1.0 exactly: the build asserts it pixel for pixel on all five hero sheets. The shader contract is in `hero_palettes.json` (`shader`) and in the docstring of `build_hero_palettes.py`, whose `Palettes.apply()` is the executable reference.

| File | Size | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_cloth.png` | 336x252 | loincloth pattern atlas: one 42 x 36 window per hero cell at (69, 62) of the 176 x 112 cell |
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_lut_blue.png` | 16x1 | hero colour 'blue' |
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_lut_gold.png` | 16x1 | hero colour 'gold' |
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_lut_green.png` | 16x1 | hero colour 'green' |
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_lut_key.png` | 16x1 | the 1.0 hero colours the palette shader replaces (alpha 0 = unused entry) |
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_lut_pink.png` | 16x1 | hero colour 'pink' |
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_lut_white.png` | 16x1 | hero colour 'white' |
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_lut_yellow.png` | 16x1 | hero colour 'yellow' (= key: the 1.0 look) |
| `sprites/player/palettes/hero_palettes.json` | - | colour names -> LUT roles (hex), slot defaults, arena swaps, pattern list, cloth window, UI colours |

LUT entries (x of the 16 x 1 texture; 10-15 unused): 0 `cloth`, 1 `cloth_shadow`, 2 `ink`, 3 `outline`, 4 `hair_a`, 5 `hair_b`, 6 `skin`, 7 `skin_shadow`, 8 `skin_dark`, 9 `cheek`.

| Colour | cloth | cloth_shadow | ink | outline | hair_a | hair_b | skin | skin_shadow | skin_dark | cheek |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| yellow | `#ffe94f` | `#f3aa39` | `#63513a` | `#272018` | `#351f21` | `#362022` | `#ffa43a` | `#f07731` | `#ec6c2f` | `#eb6439` |
| blue | `#8ae6ff` | `#3f9fe8` | `#1b4a8c` | `#142a55` | `#1f3a70` | `#1f3a70` | `#ffa43a` | `#f07731` | `#ec6c2f` | `#eb6439` |
| pink | `#c41f8f` | `#861465` | `#ffa8de` | `#3d0d33` | `#5a1650` | `#5a1650` | `#ffa43a` | `#f07731` | `#ec6c2f` | `#eb6439` |
| green | `#13893d` | `#0b5c35` | `#a6ee7c` | `#0e321d` | `#17462a` | `#17462a` | `#ffa43a` | `#f07731` | `#ec6c2f` | `#eb6439` |
| white | `#f7f4ec` | `#b7bfd2` | `#4c5a86` | `#2c2c3a` | `#3e3e52` | `#3e3e52` | `#ffa43a` | `#f07731` | `#ec6c2f` | `#eb6439` |
| gold | `#ffc928` | `#c97d10` | `#fff7c2` | `#3f2508` | `#5a360c` | `#5a360c` | `#ffa43a` | `#f07731` | `#ec6c2f` | `#eb6439` |

Slot defaults: P1 yellow / spots, P2 blue / stripes, P3 pink / zigzag, P4 green / plain. Arena swaps (E.9): jungle: green -> white, swamp: green -> white. `gold` is the 25-paintings unlock (C.9); `white` is P4 on green arenas.

| # | Pattern | Rule | Unlock |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | spots | the 1.0 leopard spots (the sheet's own ink pixels) | default |
| 1 | plain | no pattern | default |
| 2 | stripes | 2 px bands every 5 px | default |
| 3 | zigzag | 2 px zigzag band every 6 px, 8 px wavelength | default |
| 4 | checks | 4 px checkerboard | paintings_10 |
| 5 | dots | 2 x 2 dots on a staggered 5 px grid | paintings_10 |
| 6 | tiger | 2 px diagonal stripes every 6 px | paintings_10 |
| 7 | pinstripes | 2 px vertical stripes every 5 px | paintings_10 |
| 8 | diamonds | diamonds of radius 2 on an 8 px grid | paintings_10 |
| 9 | waves | 2 px rounded wave every 6 px | paintings_10 |
| 10 | sash | one 5 px diagonal sash across the cloth | paintings_10 |
| 11 | trim | 2 px border along the cloth edge | paintings_10 |

Readability checks (recomputed by every build; CIEDE2000, simulations normal/machado, protan/machado, deutan/machado, tritan/machado, protan/vienot, deutan/vienot): smallest difference between two slot defaults over all simulations: loincloth 29.0, loincloth shadow 21.9 (a value above about 20 reads as a clearly different colour). The two light cloths (yellow, blue) and the two dark ones (pink, green) differ in hue along the blue-yellow axis that red-green dichromats keep, and every pair also differs in pattern; tags, arrows and eggs use the same split (`mp_indicators.png`).

Background camouflage (share of the background pixels behind a standing hero within CIEDE2000 10 of the loincloth colour): everything under 10 % except blue on coast 13 %; white on feast 43 %; blue on ice 15 %; green on jungle 16 %; pink on mushroom 12 %; white on sky 77 %. The dark per-slot outline keeps those heroes readable (`heroes_biomes.png`); green is swapped for white on jungle / swamp arenas.

Sheets with a `rows` list hold one skin per row: the biome rows follow `LevelData.BIOMES` (jungle, cave, ice, volcano, feast, village, canyon, swamp, coast, ruins, sky), so the row of a level's biome is `LevelData.BIOMES.find(biome)`.

Faces right (air on the right): flip_h for `face=l`. Row = biome (LevelData.BIOMES order; jungle bark, cave bark, ice frost, volcano charred, feast wafer, village bark, canyon bleached, swamp fen, coast driftwood, ruins jade, sky driftwood). | `ui/countdown_stones.png` | 160x32 | 32x32 | 5x1 | (16, 32) | - | stone countdown beside an edge arrow before a hero off the view turns into an egg (D.2: 5 s Beginner, 3 s Expert). Cell = digit - 1 (0 = '1' ... 4 = '5') |
| `ui/emotes.png` | 128x32 | 32x32 | 4x1 | (16, 32) | - | emote bubble over a hero (Look double tap, D.11 / E.9): cells 0 '!', 1 '?', 2 heart, 3 angry. Pivot (16, 32) = the tail's tip: put it a few art px over the hero's head |
| `ui/hud_belt.png` | 160x32 | 32x32 | 5x1 | - | - | belt icon next to the hearts (C.1 rule 5, 16 x 16 logical): what a Swap brings. Cells: 0 club, 1 hammer, 2 axe, 3 boomerang (= the swirling axe), 4 spear |
| `ui/player_arrows.png` | 128x192 | 32x32 | 4x6 | (16, 16) | - | edge arrows for a hero outside the view (D.2) and hit-direction cues: row = hero colour in UiPlayers.PALETTE_COLOURS order (0 yellow = P1's default, 1 blue = P2, 2 pink = P3, 3 green = P4, 4 white, 5 gold), column = left, right, up, down (cell = row * 4 + column; rows 0-3 are the phase-1 slot rows unchanged). Show the stone countdown (countdown_stones.png) next to it |
| `ui/player_tags.png` | 128x336 | 32x48 | 4x7 | (16, 48) | - | P1-P4 tag + colour arrow over a hero (D.1: at stage start and whenever heroes overlap; E.9 versus round start). Pivot = arrow tip: place it about 4 art px over the hero's head. Row 0 (cells 0-3) = P1 yellow, P2 blue, P3 pink, P4 green (the slot defaults, as in phase 1); rows 1-6 = the tags in each hero colour (UiPlayers.PALETTE_COLOURS order: yellow, blue, pink, green, white, gold): the tag of slot k wearing colour c is cell (1 + c) * 4 + k - use it when a player picked another colour in the lobby or wears white on a green arena |

Notes from the hand-over (art-B's words, kept here because the staging folder is not versioned):

*Phase 3 art (art-B areas) - hand-over*

- **Islands are built from the game's own tiles.** `autotile.py` is a Python twin of LevelTiles' auto-tiling (ground index, slopes, under-slope tiles, the fill_b / bottom_b hash), so each island is laid out like a strip of level and dressed with its world's props at 1x; a 14 % haze towards the coast sky #80d6fa sets them back (per-colour blend, no filter). No new pack.
- **Bark grain over the body colour only.** The grain of `swamp/terrain_bark.png` replaces only pixels of its flat body colour #523923 in tiles 0-34, in tile-local coordinates (period 32 px both ways), so edges, outline, back wall, spikes and alpha - the collision layout of 10.1 - are untouched and every tile still joins every other one.
- **Roc feathers by colour role.** The phase-2 luminance map turned the anchor feathers' white half gold (they read as falling leaves); phase 3 swaps colours by role (vane slate, underside silver, shaft pale, tip quarter gold) for both the falling `roc_parts` feathers and the ground props `feather_a-d`.
- **Gust loop.** EnemySkin plays `gust` 5-7 at 2 ticks a frame in a loop; the swirl now moves 6 px downwind per frame with scrolling speed lines, so it reads as wind blowing outward instead of a flicker. Cell size and pivot unchanged (160 x 88, (80, 72)).

*World 5 (Sunbaked Canyon) - hand-over from art-B to art-A*

Body boxes are the bounding box of the first idle frame (as 1.0 measured them) - suggestions; the Roller's ball is about 58 x 52 art px (29 x 26 logical); the Guard's head top is 98 art px above its feet, its horn tip 109.

Tusker hit zones (logical px relative to the feet point, facing right; mirror x when it faces left; measured from idle frame 0, the same for both palettes): body x -30..+30, y -52..+0; head (weak point while dizzy / stuck) x +8..+36, y -37..-11; leafy rump (co-op weak point) x -39..-16, y -43..-3. The tusks are inside the head zone's front edge (they glance during `charge`). Unlike the 2x bosses of 1.0, Tusker is native 1x (DESIGN B.1), so its pivot is the normal `cell_h - 16`; `spin`, `roll` and `flash` balls sit on the baseline (code flies the arcs), `curl` keeps the source's hop.

Both atlases keep the exact 8 x 5 / 40-index layout and collision table of 10.1 (they are gradient maps of shipped atlases, tile for tile).

| Biome | Folder | Terrain | Palette / mood | Parallax set |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sunbaked Canyon (world 5) | `tiles/canyon` | `terrain.png` (sun-baked sandstone, straw tufts, bone-stake spikes), `terrain_mesa.png` (banded red mesa rock: walls, palisade bases, the boss arena banks), `mud_floor.png` (wallow) | sand `#d47c46` / `#e8a25d` / `#f6c97e`, mesa red `#c85e3e` / `#a8402c`, under a clear blue sky `#5ebcec` that turns cream `#fff1d6` at the horizon; rose haze spires and mesas behind, dark red-brown ridge with dusky cacti in front; saturated green cacti and red / yellow creatures for contrast; the brightest outdoor biome after the jungle | `backgrounds/canyon` layer0-3 |

Draw at y = 0 back to front and repeat horizontally, exactly like the jungle / ice / volcano sets.

- **Guard / Shellback halls are 4 rows (resolved by the orchestrator).** At the 1x DESIGN asks for, the RPG reptile stands 109 art px (about 54 logical) from feet to horn tip; its head top is 98 art px (49 logical) up, so it does not fit a 3-row (96 art px) hall (`_handover/mocks/scale_board.png`). Keeper / Guard halls are 4 rows (64 logical px) for the validator, LEVEL_DESIGN, the solo search and the level designers; a hero must still provably not pass over a guard.
- **5-1 opening and the cave bear (phase 1 additions).** `tiles/canyon/props/raft_beached.png` (the raft the heroes landed with: two rows of the shipped wood platform + rope bands; decoration, the riding raft is objects/raft), `driftwood.png`, `driftwood_log.png`, `shell_conch.png`, `shell_snail.png` for the beach of DESIGN A.3; `sprites/enemies/bear.png` (+`_b`), the Western FPS bear of the A.5 Snapper row at 2x like every WF piece: 63 x 51 logical, about 1.6 x the hero - a set-piece Snapper for a cave mouth (`mock_beach.png`, `mock_bear.png`).
- **EnemySkin rows** for every art-B sheet (worlds 5 and 6) are generated into `_handover/ENEMY_SKIN_ROWS.md` (enemies-A / enemies-B paste them into `scripts/enemies/enemy_skin.gd`).
- **WF props at 2x, not 1x.** DESIGN F.1 says WF props '1x as is', but the Western FPS pack is drawn at half the anchor's density (1 px outlines next to the anchor's 2 px). At 1x they read as thin-lined toys next to the hero; at integer 2x they match the anchor outline and the size of the 1.0 props (rock_big 64x52, skull_big 54x47). All WF props and the WF sky / mesa layers are 2x.
- **Sky from WF, not RB 17.** The far layer uses RB backgrounds/17.png as DESIGN says (towers painted out); its own sky is keyed out and the opaque sky layer is the WF sky at 2x with a banded warm horizon. The Emcee Flesher desert layers were tried by the scouts but are not used (their painterly clusters fight the anchor style), so that pack needs no credit row.
- **Walk cycles.** The RPG dino and reptile have no walk frames: `walk` = the idle frames at a higher fps (the body bobs; the code moves it). Tusker's `walk` = two charge frames at 8 fps.
- **Tusker 'rage' palette** = the source's sheet 2 (dark hide) with the mane / leaf tail / spin effect recoloured fiery red-orange, so phase 3 reads as angry rather than old (sheet 2 alone has a grey mane).
- **Mud skin** is a separate 4-tile strip (`mud_floor.png`), not a full atlas: tiles 0-2 = the `:` surface (6 px lower than ground, drawn by world-A like the tar floor), 3 = body below it.
- **Not built here (not world 5 or not art-B's):** vines / rolled vines, bark board, flower pot, x2 tablet, plates (art-A objects); tar-tribe tribesman palette (shipped `rival`, belongs with the Chieftains, P2.10); bull_rex and tortoises use shipped sheets unchanged.
- **Mocks** (`_handover/mocks/`, 640x360 + x4 copies like docs/art/mock_*.png): `mock_canyon` (5-1), `mock_beach` (5-1 opening), `mock_bear`, `mock_gulch` (5-2, with the scout's P2 colour proof, which is not a shipped file), `mock_wallow` (5-2b); `sheet_*.png` review boards with grid, frame numbers and pivots; `scale_board.png`.

*World 6 (Tar Fen) - hand-over from art-B to art-A*

| Biome | Folder | Terrain | Palette / mood | Parallax set |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tar Fen (world 6, 6-1) | `tiles/swamp` | `terrain.png` (murky moss over peat, dead-stake spikes), `terrain_bark.png` (wet mangrove bark: walls, trunks, root ledges), `tar_floor.png` (`:` in tar) | moss `#2d4223` / `#4e6630` / `#848c4c`, peat and bark `#46301e` / `#7a5a36`, tar black-violet `#1e1628` with a violet sheen `#a892c4`, under a dark teal canopy `#14201e` over fen fog `#41605a`; slimes, frogs and the hero carry the saturated colour | `backgrounds/swamp` layer0-3 (set `swamp`) |
| Spore Hollow (6-2) | `tiles/swamp` | `terrain_mushroom.png` (deep violet cap-flesh `#331d50` with glowing pink rims `#d689d8`, glowing teal spore spines) | near-black violet cave `#1c1028`; luminous teal caps `#4cc4b8` / `#94f0dc` mark the way; red and blue Puffcaps | `backgrounds/swamp/mushroom_*` (set `mushroom`) |
| Heart of the Mangrove (6-2b) | `tiles/swamp` | `terrain_bark.png` walls, `terrain.png` floor | dark heartwood `#2a1e16`, root knots `#524028`, mossy Guardian | `backgrounds/swamp/trunk_*` (set `mangrove`) |

- **Tar floor location.** world-A asked art-A for `tiles/common/tar_floor.png`; art-B's `tiles/swamp/tar_floor.png` has exactly that layout (0 top_left, 1 top, 2 top_right, 3 fill, surface 6 px lower). Either world-A reads the biome's skin (`tiles/<biome>/<x>_floor.png`: canyon mud, swamp tar) or art-A drops the staged byte-identical copy into `tiles/common/`. The `~` tar strip candidate is staged too (recolour of water.png, same layout).
- **Three backdrop sets, not one.** 6-1 (outdoor fen), 6-2 (mushroom cave) and 6-2b (inside the hollow tree) need different backdrops; the folder is `backgrounds/swamp/` for all three, the set names `swamp`, `mushroom`, `mangrove` are proposals for ParallaxSets / LevelData.BACKGROUNDS (world-A).
- **`terrain_bark` is extra.** LEVEL_DESIGN 15.2 lists `swamp/terrain` and `swamp/terrain_mushroom`; a third set for the walls inside the hollow mangrove and Old Mangrove's chamber was needed (mossy jungle walls inside a tree read wrong). Designers name it in `terrain_b`.
- **Mangrove arm from a log, not `vine_branch`.** DESIGN F.1 names the jungle `vine_branch` for the 3-segment root arm; it is 6 px thick, too thin for an arm that punches. The arm segments are cut from the Ninja Adventure mossy log (2x, turned 90 degrees, bark ramp): 26 x 32 art px each.
- **Mangrove body at 2x = 96 x 104 art px** (48 x 52 logical): it fills about 3 of the 4 wall columns (15-18) and 3.5 rows; the face (mouth and eyes) is a 20 x 20 logical box. The source has no death animation: `dead` is idle frame 0 in a withered grey ramp.
- **Puffcap sheet cell size.** The RPG mushroom file says 192 x 163; its cells are 192 x 153 (7 rows). The Puffcap is big for a snapper (51 x 42 logical body); `puffcap_b` (the source's blue sheet) glows in the dark of 6-2, `puffcap` (red) is the classic one.
- **Slime jump frames are re-anchored.** The source moves the blob inside its cell during the jump; frames 11-21 are bottom-centred on the feet point so only the code moves the body (Dropper `air` / `land`).
- **Grubs and Leeches are small** (Ninja Adventure at the required 2x: 15 x 13 logical). Fine for a crawling grub and a back-rider; a bigger Leech would need 3x, which DESIGN F.1 does not allow for Ninja art.
- **Mosquito** = shipped `insect_b` recoloured with its layout untouched (a grasshopper body: it reads as a big swamp bug). EnemySkin can map `mosquito` to the `insect` case.
- **Ceiling props keep the 1.0 ceiling names** (`moss_fringe`, `vine_a`, `vine_b`, `stalactite`, `drips`, `drip_cap` under `tiles/swamp/props/`), so the existing CEILING_PROPS lists anchor them with no code change.
- **Not built here:** the Tar Tribe palette of `rival` (with the Chieftains, P2.10), Chomper's saddle (art-A), honey / syrup skins (Feast Land, art-A), jelly flyers and the coast (world 7).
- **Mocks** (`_handover/mocks/`, 640 x 360 + x4): `mock_fen` (6-1), `mock_hollow` (6-2), `mock_mangrove` (6-2b chamber); boards `sheet_<sheet>.png`, `sheet_props_swamp.png`, `sheet_swamp_terrain.png`.

*World 7 (Coral Coast) - hand-over from art-B to art-A*

| Biome | Folder | Terrain | Palette / mood | Parallax set |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Coral Coast (world 7, 7-1) | `tiles/coast` | `terrain.png` (salmon coral rock, violet urchin spikes), `terrain_sand.png` (pale beach sand with dune grass) | sand `#d8b478` / `#f0d8a0`, coral `#b85c4c` / `#e0906a`, sea `#5ab8e8` / `#77d9ff` under the anchor's cloud sky, palms `#4a8a3a`; urchins `#5a2c74` | `backgrounds/coast` layer0-3 (set `coast`) |
| Sea Caves / Squid Grotto (7-2, 7-2b) | `tiles/coast` | `terrain_cave.png` (wet teal rock with barnacle rims) | near-black teal `#14282e`, rock `#325a54` / `#80b4a0`, glowing pink / cyan coral heads `#e870a8` / `#6ad8d8`; Inkjaw's green `#adbc3a` (red `#e0394c` in rage) | `backgrounds/coast/cave_*` (set `sea_cave`) |

- **`terrain_cave` is extra** (like `swamp/terrain_bark`): LEVEL_DESIGN 15.2 lists `coast/terrain` and `coast/terrain_sand`; the dark Sea Caves and the Squid Grotto need a wet rock set (coral pink inside a dark cave read wrong). Designers name it in `terrain_a` / `terrain_b`.
- **Coral is salmon, not magenta.** The style proof `terrain_coral.png` was candy-pink; next to the sand it read as sweets, so the ramp moved toward salmon / terracotta (`#3a1e22`-`#ffd0a4`).
- **Palms are composites.** No pack has a side-view palm: the crowns are two Ninja Adventure desert palm tops (2x, one mirrored), the ringed trunk is drawn by the pipeline in the anchor manner (three tones, a ring band, 1 px outline). The far palms of `layer3_dunes` use the same recipe, hazed.
- **Sea snail** = shipped `turtle_b` with the RPG spiral shell (item 75) laid over its hexagon shell in every frame and a grey slug body; DESIGN F.1's AP shell item 18 is a turtle shell and would change nothing. It keeps the turtle layout (EnemySkin `turtle` case).
- **Octopi and fish are small** (Ninja Adventure 16 px art at the required 2x: about 16 x 16 logical). Right for a dangler on a thread and a leaping fish; bigger would need 3x, which F.1 does not allow.
- **Jelly flyers are translucent**: art-B's slime sheet with the body at alpha 204 (outline and highlights opaque). Godot draws the partial alpha as is; nothing else in the game is see-through, so it reads as a jelly.
- **Inkjaw's tentacles** are chains of round beads cut from the RPG octopus (1x, the same 2 px outline as the 2x squid) with the inner outlines turned dark green, so a chain reads as one limb. Ready-made `tentacle_up` (7 beads, about 1 x 3.5 tiles) and `tentacle_slam` (6 beads lying), plus the three single beads to chain longer ones. The ink blob, bubbles and the slam shadow are in `inkjaw_parts.png` too (fx is art-A's folder).
- **Squid Grotto anemones**: the far layer's glowing coral heads are Ninja Adventure berry bushes recoloured (the shipped crystal props read as fangs when tinted pink).
- **Mocks** (`_handover/mocks/`, 640 x 360 + x4): `mock_shell_beach` (7-1), `mock_sea_caves` (7-2), `mock_squid_grotto` (7-2b), `mock_squid_grotto_coop` (Tentacle Lock); boards `sheet_<sheet>.png`, `sheet_props_world7.png`, `sheet_terrain_world7.png`, `scale_board_world7.png`.

*World 8 (Idol Ruins) - hand-over from art-B to art-A*

| Biome | Folder | Terrain | Palette / mood | Parallax set |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Idol Ruins (world 8, 8-1) | `tiles/ruins` | `terrain.png` (golden sandstone, bronze spikes), `terrain_jade.png` (moss-grown jade) | sandstone `#b88c44` / `#e2c26a`, jade `#3a7a64` / `#86c49c`, under a golden haze `#fce0a8` with hazy step pyramids; deep jungle shade `#1e402e` in front | `backgrounds/ruins` layer0-3 (set `ruins`) |
| Hall of Idols / Idol Court (8-2, 8-2b) | `tiles/ruins` | `terrain.png` walls and floors | dark plum-brown temple shadow `#261e20` behind golden stone; braziers; the Sun Idol gold `#d09e3c`, the Moon Idol jade `#3a7a62`, asleep = grey stone | `backgrounds/ruins/temple_*` (set `temple`) |

- **No RB 21 / 22 band.** DESIGN F.1 names RPG backdrops 21 / 22 for the ruins; they are perspective paintings of a castle wall that do not cut into a side-view band. The far layer is two stepped pyramids built from the new temple tiles and hazed, over the shipped far hills; the near layers are the shipped jungle hills / forest in sunlit and deep greens with mossy Ninja Adventure columns.
- **The Mimic is built from the container chest, not from the RPG mimic.** objects-A draws `objects/container skin=chest` with the shipped `sprites/objects/chest.png` (cell 0 closed), and DESIGN A.5 wants the Mimic to look exactly like it; the RPG mimic (F.1's pick) is three times bigger and in another style. So `idle` is that chest cell pixel for pixel (a test pins it), and the Mimic's tells are pipeline pixel edits on the chest's own cells: the shudder, two glowing eyes in the lifted lid's gap, white fangs, a red tongue, the lunge.
- **Twin Idols** keep the Colossus frame numbers and add a `sleep` row (the idle frames in grey stone: asleep and armoured) - grid 6 x 4. The Moon Idol is pre-mirrored (faces right, wall strip on the left, pivot (0, 208)) so the code needs no flip; the Sun Idol keeps the Colossus pivot (260, 208). Rage frames 14-15 glow red-orange (Sun) / cyan (Moon). Place each pivot on the screen edge with a 2-column wall, as the Colossus.
- **Shaman** = the anchor's npc dragon-man (D.7), 1x, its idle loop as idle / walk / cast; the bone-shield effect it casts is fx (art-A).
- **Mocks** (`_handover/mocks/`, 640 x 360 + x4): `mock_overgrown_steps` (8-1), `mock_hall_of_idols` (8-2), `mock_idol_court` (8-2b); boards `sheet_<sheet>.png`, `sheet_props_world8.png`, `sheet_terrain_world8.png`, `scale_board_world8.png`.

*World 9 (Sky Spire) - hand-over from art-B to art-A*

| Biome | Folder | Terrain | Palette / mood | Parallax set |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sky Spire (world 9, 9-1) | `tiles/sky` | `terrain.png` (lavender cloud-rock, pale violet storm crystals), `terrain_rock.png` (storm slate) | cloud `#c6c0f2` / `#f2ecff`, slate `#4c5672` / `#96a2bc` under a high blue `#82b0f0` with far sky islands | `backgrounds/sky` layer0-3 (set `sky`) |
| Thunderhead / Roc's Spire / Storm Nest (9-1b, 9-2, 9-2b) | `tiles/sky` | both sets | slate-violet thunderhead `#363852`, dark clouds `#3e4058`; the Roc storm slate `#4e5a76` with a gold crest `#e0b030`; lightning white-yellow | `backgrounds/sky/storm_*` (set `storm`) |
| Chieftains' Pyre (9-3) | `tiles/sky` | `terrain_rock.png` | the anchor's sunset (orange sky, yellow sun), dusk-violet islands and clouds; tar-black Tar Tribe camp; Gorm tar-black + bone, Gulla ochre + red | `backgrounds/sky/pyre_*` (set `pyre`) |

- **Three backdrop sets** (`sky`, `storm`, `pyre`) from the same four layers: 9-1 climbs in the clear, 9-1b / 9-2 / Storm Nest under the thunderhead, the final fight at sunset (AP `sky-2.png`).
- **Sky islands at 4x** on the farthest layer only (F.1), hazed into the sky so the chunky pixels read as distance.
- **The Roc's re-pack**: the 2x sheet is 4 x 4 cells of 288 x 240 = 1152 x 960 (an 8 x 2 strip would be 2304 px wide). Frame numbers = the shipped pterodactyl's (EnemySkin: an own case, cell 288 x 240, pivot (144, 208)). The gold crest is a mask edit: the head's orange line (within 30 px of the beak tip, 13 px of the eye in the screech frames) turned gold - a gold head and beak that read at every angle.
- **Lightning** is the RPG fx zigzag (F.1) turned upright, white-yellow: `roc_lightning.png` holds one segment (its size is in the sheet's note) and its mirror - stack them alternately down the marked column for any height - and a white flash frame; the darkening cloud that marks the column 22 ticks ahead is `storm_mark` in `roc_parts.png`. The `zones/lightning` owner (9-1b, Cloud Top) can use the same files.
- **Chieftains** grow a taller cell for the dino-skull headdress (Gorm: bone skull 57, Gulla: red-stained skull 58). They keep rival's frame order but not its cell (96 x 96, pivot (48, 80): 16 px more headroom). Roll, hurt and lying frames are bare-headed (the skull flies off on a hit). War paint = rival's darker skin shade in bone / red; their leaf tunic becomes ash-bone (Gorm) or red leather (Gulla).
- **Storm crystals, not gold shards**: the spike tiles and crystal props are pale violet; gold shards read as bananas next to the food.
- **Tar Tribe camp** props (9-3) are shipped village / Ninja Adventure camp pieces in tar-black hide; the pyre is three Ninja Adventure logs and the RPG fx flame.
- **Mocks** (`_handover/mocks/`, 640 x 360 + x4): `mock_cloudbreak` (9-1), `mock_thunderhead` (9-1b / 9-2, storm set), `mock_storm_nest` (9-2b), `mock_chieftains_pyre` (9-3, pyre set); boards `sheet_<sheet>.png`, `sheet_props_world9.png`, `sheet_terrain_world9.png`, `scale_board_world9.png`.


### 17.7 Edit log (2.0)

| File | Owner | Source | Edits |

### 17.9 Known gaps (2.0, art-A)

- See-saw: the wood plank (lengths 3-6) and its mushroom-cap and ice-floe skins (same layout); other biomes use the wood plank.
- Drawn by the pipeline in the anchor palette (no pack has them): the pulley wheel and rope, the revive egg's oval, the petroglyph figures of the x2 tablet and the cave-painting glyphs, the vine coil, the geyser rim and jet, the raft's log ends, the cookpot's bowl and mouth, the spawn slab and the crown; everything else is recoloured / composited pack art.
- `hero_palettes.json` is reference data (shader contract, colour names, UI colours, pattern list). If `hero_palette.gd` (player-A) reads it at runtime, check that the export carries it (an `include_filter` entry by core-A); otherwise keep the few values it needs as constants. The LUT / atlas PNGs are ordinary textures.
- Arena thumbnails (`ui/arena/thumb_<arena id>.png`, 120 x 66) are not drawn: the arenas are DA's level files (still changing in phase 3) and ui-A's arena select draws a mini map of each file in its biome's colours.
- The arena side frames (`ui/arena/frame_<biome>.png`, ten with phase 3's canyon and sky) exist for screens wider than 20 cells (DESIGN E.5), but no script draws them yet (asked of world-B, wf9_art_to_world-B.txt).
- The versus corner panels are one arena row (32 px) tall: the 2x Ninja Adventure portrait (E.9) does not fit, so they get `ui/portrait_heads.png` (28 px heads painted from the 1.0 HUD head's silhouette); the Ninja portrait (`ui/portraits.png`) is for the lobby, the results and the join panel.
- Feast Land D / E use the shipped feast parallax (`backgrounds/feast`, outside art-A's and art-B's globs) with their own terrains and props (17.14).
- Belt icons crop the handle end of the longer weapons (club, spear) at the cell edge; the heads stay whole.
- The egg's hatch frames show the hero curled in the 1.0 spots (the pattern step is off for non-hero sheets).
- The cave-painting item (`sprites/items/painting.png`) shows one of six glyphs (index % 6) on its fragment; the painting's own picture is `ui/paintings.png` cell `index`, and its piece of the mural is the rect of `ui/mural.png` given in 17.13.
- Chomper's saddle: `sprites/objects/rex_saddle.png` is an overlay on the shipped rex sheets (same grid); the riders themselves are the hero sheets drawn by code.
- Phase 3: the railed raft's fence (`sprites/objects/raft_rails.png`) is an overlay that objects-B's raft.gd must draw over raft.png (closed / open towards the bank that stopped it, G45); until it does, a railed raft looks like a plain one. Feast Land E's custard ':' floor is `tiles/feast/syrup_floor.png` (world-A's biome-first lookup); every other feast + syrup level - the Sky Picnic arena - draws it too.

### 17.11 Versus art (DESIGN E.3 / E.8 / E.9)

The cookpot, the spawn pads, the crown and the stack pictures of the G1 slice (Totem Ring, Grub Stack), and phase 2's who-is-who and screen art: portraits and corner-panel heads in every hero colour and three expressions, the round sundial, the medal discs of the awards and tally medals, the scoreboard plate, the results cave wall with the heroes painted on it. The Golden Drumstick item is in 17.4, the hit sparks in the attacker's colour (`sprites/fx/hit_stars_players.png`) in 17.4 too. Colour rows follow `UiPlayers.PALETTE_COLOURS` (yellow, blue, pink, green, white, gold); the tags and arrows of 17.5 have a row per colour as well.

### 17.12 Audio (audio owner's hand-over, DESIGN F.2)

Notes from the audio hand-over (the audio owner's words: the music budget and the decisions and flags of each batch, kept here because the staging folder is not versioned):

*Audio batch 1 - hand-over from the audio owner to core-A (AudioTable) and art-A (manifest, credits)*

- **Scope**: the whole effects row of DESIGN F.2 (PLAN P1.1 makes AudioTable batch 1 = effects), not only the slice's list, so that no 2.0 effect name is left without a row (`Audio.play_sfx` of a name without a row is an error). Music: only what the slice plays; the rest of F.2's music (worlds 5-9, bosses, battle B / C, sudden-death loop) is batch 2 (P2.6 / P2.11).
- **Variants with a meaning**: `DUO_HOP` has two files: variant 0 `duo_hop_a.wav` = the boost (Shoulder Hop, Totem launch; F.2's Junkala interaction16), variant 1 `duo_hop_b.wav` = the carry (a rider lands on the carrier; MoxieCat lift). Callers pass the variant (`Audio.play_sfx(Sfx.DUO_HOP, 0 / 1)`); without it the two alternate. `RAFT_SPLASH` (2) and `BAT_HIT` (3) are plain variety variants (cycle).
- **Shared files**: `drum_beat_a.wav` plays `DRUM`, `COUNT_IN` and `HOT_ROCK_FUSE` (F.2 names Junkala Blip5 for all three; the count-in now beats the very sound the drums make); `splash_heavy_a.wav` plays `SPLASH_HEAVY` and `GEYSER_SPOUT`. A shared file has one volume (tools/audio_loudness.py keys volumes by file).
- **Round win**: `MUSIC_ROUND_WIN` is MintoDog "Stage Clear Short" (F.2's second pick, sibling of the shipped `level_complete_a.ogg`, 3.3 s, carries the scoreboard). E.8's 0.5 s Junkala `fanfare1` gong is not shipped: it would need its own `Sfx` name (a contract request to core-A) and is too short to carry the scoreboard.
- **Not in batch 1: `MUSIC_VERSUS_SUDDEN_DEATH`** (F.2: Wolfgang_ "8-Bit Battle Loop"). It cannot be loop-fixed from the published render: fitted to its 180 BPM grid (190 of 203 onsets, 0.85 ms residual) the 26.65 s file is 22.7 ms short of 80 beats, the music stops 44 ms before the next downbeat and starts after 17 ms of silence, and the track has no rest anywhere else (a dropout plus an early downbeat at every seam); it has no sample-exact internal repeat to cut a loop from either. Batch 2: re-render the author's `famitracker_sources/BattleTheme.ftm` with its loop, or take F.2's runner-up (Junkala Chiptune Heroes "dangerous encounter A (faster)"). core-A's stand-in row stays until then. Battle tracks B / C are batch 2.
- **Chomper bite**: `chomper_bite_a.wav` already holds the growl (dino_voice_b) and the chomp, so a caller plays `CHOMPER_BITE` alone (objects-B's `mount.gd` does); no extra `ENEMY_VOICE`.
- **Countdown**: `COUNTDOWN_BEEP` is one kheetor beep (played three times); `COUNTDOWN_GO` is Junkala `fanfare2` (E.8: no adult voice). kheetor's own higher 'go' beep is not used (the file cuts it off mid-reverb).
- **Spear stick**: the F.2 pick (Spring Spring snd_enemyland) is a sub-bass thump (83 % of its energy under 100 Hz), inaudible on phone speakers; it is layered with the shipped wood knock `club_hit_wood_a.wav` and plays at the scenery-hit level of 1.0 (-2 LU). `BRACE` keeps the plain thump (40 Hz high-pass).
- **Limited files** (true-peak limiter, the least that reaches the target): `tar_glug_a.wav` 8.5 dB off the highest peaks (peak-to-loudness 23.2 -> 14.7 dB); `bat_hit_c.wav` 0.9 dB off the highest peaks (peak-to-loudness 13.1 -> 12.7 dB); `crowd_cheer_a.ogg` 9.8 dB off the highest peaks (peak-to-loudness 21.5 -> 14.7 dB); `level_canyon_a.ogg` 2.4 dB off the highest peaks (peak-to-loudness 18.4 -> 16.4 dB). In the glug and the cheer only 0.1 % or less of the samples (isolated spikes, clap tips) are above the limiter line; unlimited they would play 6-8 LU under their target at the -1 dBTP cap. The canyon theme's limiting is F.2's 'limit'.
- **Listen-through pending** (P4.4): nothing was auditioned by ear (no audio output on the pipeline machine). Priority for the listen: the canyon theme's limiting, `spear_stick_a` (layer balance), `bat_hit_*` (crack + NES whoosh), `crowd_cheer_a` (limited claps), `countdown_beep_a` against `countdown_go_a` (two authors back to back).
- **Meter note for core-A** (no action needed for batch 1): `tools/audio_loudness.py` measures a mono file as one channel, while Godot plays a mono stream at full level on both speakers, so every mono file (most 1.0 effects and 26 of these 40) sounds about 3 dB louder than its number next to stereo files. The 1.0 mix already has this bias throughout; batch 1 follows the tool so the two stay consistent.
- **Boot preload**: `Audio.preload_sfx()` now also loads the 33 batch-1 effect files (2.29 MB on disk; the longest WAV is 2.54 s, the 8.3 s applause stays Vorbis).

*Audio batch 2 - hand-over from the audio owner to core-A (AudioTable) and art-A (manifest, credits)*

DESIGN F.2 asks to cut the nene boss tracks to their loop regions "(music budget)"; the budget, as checked by `test_audio_batch2.py`: the 1.0 music is 21 files, 31.0 MB, 926 s (1.48 MB per track, the largest `boss_final_a.ogg` 3.55 MB). Every 2.0 music file stays at most 110 s and 3.55 MB (the largest 1.0 track); the 30 2.0 music files together stay under 30 x the 1.0 average (44.4 MB); the nene loops are at most 90 s. Published OGGs above 256 kbps are re-encoded at q0.6 (the project's transcode quality); the others are byte-identical copies. Result: batch 2 music 35.04 MB (largest file 2.42 MB, longest 106 s), all 2.0 music (batch 1 + 2) 41.42 MB for 30 files (1.38 MB per track); with the 1.0 music the game ships 72.5 MB of music. The five nene renders alone would have been 25.2 MB as q0.6 transcodes (their staged ogg_converted/ copies), the boss loops are 8.7 MB.

- **Nene boss tracks** (F.2: "cut to 60-90 s loop regions"): each published render plays one loop several times (per-bar spectral similarity 0.98-1.00 at the repeat lag; the WAV masters were re-fetched, the staged OGGs are transcodes of them). #1 (Tusker) is a 24-bar loop played 4 times, #6 (Storm Roc) a 24-bar loop twice, #2 (Mangrove) a 4-bar intro and a 32-bar loop 4 times: one pass of each is the whole composition (50.9 / 49.8 / 52.9 s) - shorter than 60 s, but a longer region would only repeat the same music inside the file. #3 (Chieftains) plays a 24-bar loop 5 times in three variations and #4 (Inkjaw) a 20-bar theme in three variations: their regions take two different variations (79.4 / 84.2 s). The renders' 3.3-3.6 min (5-6 MB each) became 1.3-2.4 MB.
- **Seam splices**: where a region is cut out of a render (nene, Typhoon's Theme, Go), the loop starts with the render's own continuation of the region's last frame and crossfades (2-20 ms) into the region's first bar at the moment the two takes agree best; the seam itself is then exactly what the composer's render plays.
- **6-2 Haunted House: F.2's 'trim the 123 ms tail' is NOT applied**: the master is 112 beats at 150 BPM within 3 ms; the 120 ms of silence at the end is the last beat's staccato rest (the track has 139 ms rests inside); trimming it would make every pass 120 ms short of its bar.
- **9-1b Typhoon's Theme v3**: F.2 says 'trim the 82 ms lead'; the render also ends with the first 84 ms of the next pass's downbeat (cut off by a fade). The loop is the region from the first sound to that second downbeat (104 beats, matched by the attack envelope), spliced on it.
- **Tallbeard export tails**: Penultimate (8-2) and Go (battle C) play at exactly 140 / 170 BPM but are 13.7 / 12.9 ms longer than 152 / 200 beats; cut to the exact length (Penultimate's extra is a near-silent tail, dropped; Go's extra is the start of the next pass, spliced). Connected (batch 1), Out of Time and Pixel War 1 are 3-10 ms longer than their nominal bars too, but their music runs 0.01 % slow by the same onset fit (a uniform resampling: the file IS the loop) - kept as published.
- **9-1 Upbeat Overworld**: kept as published (no gap, no click at the seam); its FamiTracker tempo is not exactly constant, so its bar-exactness is only known to +/-20 ms (the test allows 25 ms). First item for the listen-through.
- **Sudden death**: F.2's Wolfgang_ "8-Bit Battle Loop" cannot loop (batch 1: 22.7 ms short of 80 beats, a dropout at the seam, no internal repeat; a loop offset cannot lengthen a loop that is too short, and re-rendering the author's .ftm needs FamiTracker, a Windows GUI tracker with no headless export). F.2's runner-up ships: Junkala Chiptune Heroes "dangerous encounter A (faster)" (designed as a loop: 32 beats at 97.5 BPM to 0.2 ms; 19.7 s, the round's last minute or less).
- **Limited** (the least circular true-peak limiting that lets a loop reach -18 LUFS under -1 dBTP, as the canyon theme): `level_spore_a.ogg` 1.2 dB off the highest peaks (peak-to-loudness 17.6 -> 16.4 dB); `level_sky_climb_a.ogg` 1.3 dB off the highest peaks (peak-to-loudness 17.6 -> 16.4 dB); `level_storm_glide_a.ogg` 1.4 dB off the highest peaks (peak-to-loudness 17.8 -> 16.4 dB). Battle C's +2.0 dBTP (F.2 'limit') and the Idols' hot master (F.2 'vol -12') need no limiting: their peak-to-loudness ratios are 15 / 7.7 dB, so the AudioTable volume (-1.4 / -12.0 dB) keeps them under -1 dBTP.
- **Lightning (`Sfx.LIGHTNING_STRIKE`, core-A's phase-2 name; F.2 has no pick)**: MoxieCat's 8-bit `lightningstrike` (CC0, the pack of the batch-1 curl and bat whoosh), an NES noise crash: lead silence cut, kept for 1.5 s with a 500 ms fade (the published file decays for 2.2 s and carries 2.2 s of silence), so repeated bolts (the Roc's phase 3, Cloud Top) do not pile up. A realistic thunder (rubberduck `sfx100v2_thunder_01`) was the alternative; it does not match the chiptune mix. Proposed F.2 row for the lead designer: "lightning bolt: MoxieCat lightningstrike".
- **Round gong (new name `Sfx.ROUND_GONG`)**: E.8 / F.2's Junkala `fanfare1` at the gong that ends a round (byte-identical copy, 0.52 s). Batch 1 could not ship it: it needed a new `Sfx` name (contract file, core-A) and a caller; core-A added `Sfx.ROUND_GONG` and `Flow.end_round` plays it once per round, before the deciding moment / scoreboard.
- **Battle B / C**: an arena that names a battle track plays the round's track instead (core-A's `VersusMatch.round_music`: A, B, C in turn from round to round; `Flow._play_round_music`), so all three battle loops are heard in a match whatever the arena names.
- **Listen-through pending** (P4.4): nothing was auditioned by ear. Priority: `level_sky_climb_a` (seam timing), the five nene splices (crossfade between two takes), `level_storm_glide_a` and `versus_battle_c` (splices on the next pass's start), the limited `level_spore_a` / `level_sky_climb_a` / `level_storm_glide_a`, the sudden-death runner-up's mood.
- **Meter note** (as batch 1): mono files (5 of these 25) sound about 3 dB louder than their number next to stereo files in Godot; the 1.0 mix has the same bias.


### 17.13 The Far Shore map page and the Cave Paintings (DESIGN A.1, C.9)

Book II's map page and the pictures of the 30 Cave Paintings for ui-A's map, slab and unlocks screen. The painting slab is screen UI in the lower-right corner of the map screen (world_map.gd), not part of the map picture.

| File | Size | Frame | Grid | Use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ui/mural.png` | 144x80 | 144x80 | 1x1 | the Cave Painting mural (C.9): the story of Book II painted on the slab - the homecoming feast, the Storm Roc carrying off the Great Roast with Gorm and Gulla, the raft, the five lands and their guardians, the last stand at the pyre and the feast at home. 30 pieces of 24 x 16 (`pieces`): painting i shows the rect (i % 6 * 24, i / 6 * 16, 24, 16) in the slab's socket i once found. The whole mural is the last picture of The Long Raft Home when all 30 are found (draw it at an integer scale, 2x = 288 x 160 or 3x = 432 x 240, ideally inside painting_slab.png at the same scale) |
| `ui/painting_slab.png` | 220x104 | 220x104 | 1x1 | the Cave Painting slab (A.1 / C.9): screen UI in the lower-right corner of the Far Shore map page (world_map.gd) and on the unlocks screen. Socket of painting i: `mural_at` + (i % 6 * 24, i / 6 * 16), 24 x 16 - draw the same rect of ui/mural.png there when Save.has_painting(i). `plate` = the count plate (x, y, w, h) for '17/30' in the small font; `icons` = the top-left of the six 24 x 24 unlock marks (reward order of ui/unlock_icons.png): draw that sheet's row-1 cell over a mark when the reward is open |
| `ui/paintings.png` | 960x32 | 32x32 | 30x1 | the 30 Cave Paintings as pictures (C.9; ui-A's slab, unlocks screen and mural): cell = painting index 0..29, 32 x 32, ochre on transparent - draw them on stone. `themes` gives each index the level whose story it suits (0-19 the Book II levels in campaign order, 20-29 the Book I co-op secrets); the designers' index choice wins. ui/mural.png is the alternative where the 30 pieces assemble one picture |
| `ui/unlock_icons.png` | 144x48 | 24x24 | 6x2 | the six rewards of the Cave Paintings (C.9, Save.UNLOCK_*), column = reward: 0 mesa_rodeo (5 paintings), 1 loincloths (10), 2 variants (15), 3 cloud_top (20), 4 spear_party (25), 5 mural (30). Row 0 = locked (carved; the slab already shows these), row 1 = unlocked: draw cell 6 + k over the slab's mark k (painting_slab.png `icons`) once Save.is_unlocked(reward); also for the unlocks screen and the versus menu |
| `ui/world_map_far_shore.png` | 1280x360 | 1280x360 | 1x1 | Book II's map page (A.1, ui-A P2.8): the second 1280 x 360 page, east of the home islands, drawn like world_map_background.png (SKY_COLOR / SEA_COLOR fill beyond it). Islands left to right: 5 the red mesa, 6 the tar delta with the giant mangrove, 7 the coral coast with its sea stacks, 8 the idol isle with its temple gate, 9 the spire into the storm; the speck on the far-left horizon is home. `markers` (map px, by 'world-stage') = the 11 stops on sand or grass with their number plates clear (world_map.gd MARKERS_B2); every marker y is at most 238, so the sea under y 262 stays free for the painting slab that world_map.gd draws as screen UI in the lower-right corner |

### 17.14 Feast Land D / E skins (DESIGN A.3)

Honey Falls (`bonus_d`) and Pudding Lagoon (`bonus_e`): two terrain atlases in the 40-tile layout and collision of 10.1, and `props/feast/<name>` pictures (`assets/tiles/feast/props/`, no collision). Their liquids (`honey`, `syrup`), the honey ':' floor, the soda geyser and the wafer raft are phase-1 files (17.3, 17.10); phase 3 adds Pudding Lagoon's custard ':' floor (`tiles/feast/syrup_floor.png`, found before the berry-syrup floor of 17.10 in a feast-biome level).

| File | Size | Pivot | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tiles/feast/props/cherries.png` | 41x25 | (20, 25) | `props/feast/cherries` (Feast Land D / E dressing, A.3): a pile of six red berries (anchor item 31); pivot = bottom-centre on the floor |
| `tiles/feast/props/comb_chunk.png` | 41x26 | (20, 26) | `props/feast/comb_chunk` (Feast Land D / E dressing, A.3): a broken piece of honeycomb (five hexagonal cells); pivot = bottom-centre on the floor |
| `tiles/feast/props/cream_swirl.png` | 24x23 | (12, 23) | `props/feast/cream_swirl` (Feast Land D / E dressing, A.3): a whipped-cream swirl; pivot = bottom-centre on the floor |
| `tiles/feast/props/honey_drips_a.png` | 32x21 | (16, 0) | `props/feast/honey_drips_a` (Feast Land D / E dressing, A.3): honey running off a ledge: crust and three drops (hang it from the cell top under a ledge, `layer=front`); pivot = top-centre (it hangs) |
| `tiles/feast/props/honey_drips_b.png` | 32x27 | (16, 0) | `props/feast/honey_drips_b` (Feast Land D / E dressing, A.3): honey drips, two long drops; pivot = top-centre (it hangs) |

| `tiles/feast/props/jelly_green.png` | 31x27 | (15, 27) | `props/feast/jelly_green` (Feast Land D / E dressing, A.3): three lime jelly cubes; pivot = bottom-centre on the floor |
| `tiles/feast/props/jelly_pink.png` | 31x27 | (15, 27) | `props/feast/jelly_pink` (Feast Land D / E dressing, A.3): three pink jelly cubes; pivot = bottom-centre on the floor |

### 17.15 2.0 uses of shipped 1.0 files

Shipped 1.0 files (sections 3-12, unchanged) that 2.0 code draws for a new purpose:

| File | 2.0 use |
|---|---|
| `sprites/objects/chest.png` | `objects/container skin=chest` (objects-A): cell 0 closed, cells 1-3 opening once at 10 fps; the Mimic's disguise (sprites/enemies/mimic.png `idle`, art-B, 17.6) is built from the same cells, so a Mimic looks exactly like the chest (DESIGN A.5) |
| `sprites/items/giant_bonus.png` | Book II's `items/trophy` - the Great Roast of 9-3 Chieftains' Pyre - draws cell 0 |
| `sprites/objects/platform_wood.png` | the layout copied by the 2.0 skins platform_driftwood.png and platform_cloud.png (17.3) |
| `ui/hud_lives_icon.png` | the head of the versus corner-panel portraits ui/portrait_heads.png (17.11) |
| `backgrounds/cave/layer0_wall.png` | recoloured into the versus results backdrop ui/cave_wall.png (17.11) |
